📅 Oct 14, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Tender cabbage, savory ground beef, and a spicy, garlicky sauce make this Cabbage Stir Fry an instant favorite. Made with fresh vegetables and loaded with flavor, this 30-minute meal is an easy alternative to Chinese takeout.

Stir fries are a frequent fixture of my weekly dinner menus and stir fry noodles with chicken and vegetables is another one I make often. This week, since I had ground beef in the freezer, I switched things up and made a beefy cabbage version.

Cabbage Stir Fry

Similar to my egg roll skillet and sausage egg roll in a bowl, this has the ground meat, shredded cabbage, onion, and garlic you’d find inside a typical egg roll, and I’ve kicked up the flavor here with the punch of chili paste.

I expected the beef to be my favorite part of this stir fry. And as much as I enjoyed the savory meat, it’s the cabbage that kept me coming back for more. Cabbage is inexpensive and versatile, and when it’s sliced into noodle-like ribbons here, it’s perfect for lapping up all that incredible sauce.

I’m always amazed by how easy it is to turn an ordinary vegetable into a crave-worthy dish with just a few simple steps. I can’t wait for you to try this one.

Ingredients and Substitutions

The Ground Meat – I used ground beef here, but ground pork, turkey, and chicken will work well, too.

The Cabbage – You want green cabbage with dense, hearty leaves here.

The Aromatics – Fresh garlic, ginger, and yellow onion provide the flavor base for the stir fry.

The Sauce – Soy sauce, chili paste, and cornstarch create a slightly spicy, salty, savory sauce that thickens as it cooks.

The Garnishes – Green onions and sesame seeds provide the finishing touch for cabbage stir fry.

Notes on Equipment

  • Chef’s knife. Food prep is so much easier when you have a quality knife. It was over 10 years ago when I first tried this one. The price simply can’t be beat for quality.
  • Cutting board
  • Measuring cups and spoons
  • Wooden saute paddle. You can use any wooden or metal spatula you like, but I find that I reach for this super sturdy wooden utensil more often than not.
  • Large skillet. I love this enamel cast iron skillet for stir fries and skillet meals like this one. It goes from the stove to the table and it wipes clean like a dream.

How to Make Cabbage Stir Fry

Getting Things Ready: Before I start cooking anything, I prep the vegetables. Shred the cabbage, chop the onion, mince the garlic and the ginger.

Browning the Beef: I warm a large skillet over medium-high heat, and then add the ground beef. Cook the ground beef, crumbling it a bit after it begins to brown.

Adding the Aromatics: You’ll add the onion, garlic, and ginger to the beef and continue cooking and stirring the mixture until the onion has softened and the skillet is fragrant.

Mixing the Sauce: While the beef is cooking, I combine the soy sauce, chili paste, and cornstarch in a liquid measuring cup or bowl and whisk well to combine it.

Adding the Sauce: Pour the stir fry sauce into the skillet with the meat. Give it a good stir and scrape off any bits of beef that may have stuck to the pan.

Cooking the Cabbage: Now, we’ll add the cabbage to the skillet. Stir to mix the cabbage throughout the meat and then put a lid on the skillet. Let the cabbage steam until it looks slightly wilted.

Finishing the Stir Fry: I’ll take the lid off of the skillet once the cabbage begins to wilt and continue cooking the stir-fry and stirring occasionally, until the sauce thickens.

Garnishing the Stir Fry: I sprinkle the stir-fry with thinly sliced green onions and sesame seeds before portioning it into bowls. And I’ll give it an additional drizzle of Sriracha, if desired, for a bit of heat.

Expert Tip

Whenever I make a stir fry, I prep every ingredient and line them up next to the stove before I turn on the heat. Once you start cooking, everything happens FAST. Being prepared helps you avoid accidentally burning the beef because you got distracted chopping cabbage.

Taste for Salt – I always taste my dishes as I’m cooking. It’s especially important here if you’re using something other than ground beef. When I made this with ground pork, I found I needed to add a bit more salt, and some extra chili paste too, to achieve the same depth of flavor as the beef.

Make It Spicier – If you love spicy heat, this cabbage stir fry is practically begging to be drizzled with Sriracha. Other sauces like Bang Bang totally belong here too.

Avoid Overcooked Cabbage – I remove the lid at the end of cooking, not only to reduce the sauce, but also to keep the cabbage tender-crisp instead of soggy.

Serving Suggestions

Serve this with bowls of Chinese Egg Drop Soup for a fantastic meal. If you want to round things out with a salad, I recommend trying my Asian Cabbage Salad or Harvest House Chop Salad. And I’ll never turn down an opportunity to serve my Orange Parsley Salad, that one still makes me grin all these years later.

Storage and Reheating

How to Store: Transfer any leftovers to an airtight container. Store it in the refrigerator and enjoy within 2-3 days.

How to Reheat: Single portions will reheat well in the microwave. I recommend covering the plate with a paper towel before heating to keep the beef moist. If I’m heating up a larger batch, then I use a skillet on the stove at low heat and add a splash of water to refresh the sauce.

  • Pepper Pork Stir Fry

Frequently Asked Questions

How can I make this vegetarian?

To make a meatless cabbage stir fry, swap the ground meat for textured vegetable protein (TVP). I would add additional salt to taste if using TVP.

Can I use other kinds of cabbage?

Green cabbage works best for this recipe because it’s thicker and holds up well to the sauce. I wouldn’t recommend using varieties with thinner leaves, like napa.

Is this recipe gluten free?

It depends. Many soy sauces contain gluten. To make this gluten free, be sure to use a gluten free soy sauce or tamari.

How can I make this less spicy?

For a less spicy version, reduce the amount of chili paste in the sauce. If you don’t want any spice at all, leave the chili paste out entirely.

Cabbage Stir Fry

This Cabbage Stir Fry with ground beef, tender cabbage, and a spicy garlic sauce is an easy 30-minute dinner that beats Chinese takeout. Course Main Course Cuisine American Prep Time 10 minutes minutes Cook Time 20 minutes minutes Total Time 30 minutes minutes Servings 6 Calories 243kcal Author Mary Younkin

Ingredients

  • 1 pound ground beef (see note below for substitutions)
  • 1 medium yellow onion chopped into ½-inch pieces about 1 cup
  • 4 cloves garlic minced
  • 1 tablespoon minced ginger
  • ¼ cup low sodium soy sauce
  • 1 tablespoon chili paste or Sriracha hot sauce
  • 1 teaspoon cornstarch
  • 1 medium head green cabbage shredded thin, about 8-10 cups worth
  • ½ teaspoon kosher salt
  • ¼ teaspoon freshly ground black pepper
  • 4 green onions sliced thin
  • black and / or white sesame seeds
  • optional Bang Bang sauce or additional Sriracha

Instructions

  • In a large skillet over medium-high heat, cook and crumble the ground beef. Add the onion, the garlic, and the ginger and continue cooking and stirring for about 3 minutes until softened and fragrant.
  • While the beef and onion are cooking, whisk together the soy sauce, chili paste, and cornstarch in a small glass or bowl. Set aside. Pour in the sauce and stir to mix throughout.
  • Stir in the cabbage and reduce the heat to medium. Cover with a lid for about 3 minutes, until it begins to soften. Uncover and continue cooking uncovered for an additional minute or two, as needed, until the sauce has mostly been absorbed and the cabbage is tender-crisp.
  • Taste the stir fry and adjust the salt and pepper, as desired. Sprinkle with green onions and sesame seeds before serving. Drizzle with additional sauces, if desired.

Video

Notes

Ground chicken, ground turkey, or ground pork may all be substituted here. TVP is also a great option if that’s more your style. Regardless of your choice, you’ll want to taste before serving, because this recipe is written specifically for ground beef. When I tested it with ground chicken and pork, I needed a bit more salt in order to hit the same flavor profile. So, adjust as desired for your tastes.

📅 Oct 14, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Bourbon Chicken is one of our favorite easy Chinese Takeout meals to make at home on busy nights. Tender, moist chicken bites slathered in sweet and spicy homemade bourbon sauce are totally irresistible! If you have been craving the sticky, glossy bourbon chicken from the mall food court, this is the recipe you’re looking for. Bite-size chicken breast gets browned in a skillet, then simmered in a sauce made with brown sugar, soy sauce, apple juice, garlic, a pinch of red pepper flakes, and a splash of bourbon. The sauce cooks down into a sweet and savory glaze that clings to every piece. I guarantee you’ll simply love this one-pan, comforting meal that you can enjoy with the whole family!

5-Star Review

“Delicious and easy to make. Followed as written including the bourbon. Next time will garnish with some scallions and perhaps a few (chopped) red peppers.” -TR

Better Than Takeout, and Made in One Skillet

I never thought Asian-inspired foods would be easier to cook at home than picking up takeout. Homemade bourbon chicken is healthier (I know exactly what’s in it), freshly made, needs minimal preparation, and tastes so much better!

This mildly spicy, one-pot meal makes a perfect family dinner on busy nights. It takes about an hour from start to finish, and it serves 6.

When I tested this recipe, I found that the sauce was still quite thin at the 20-minute mark. So I added an optional cornstarch step. It takes less than a minute and gives you that thick, glossy glaze every time. You’ll know the sauce is ready when you drag a spoon across the bottom of the pan and the sauce briefly parts before flowing back together.

Asian-inspired meals are my go-to whenever I need something fast, and my kids drool over this recipe. When they want something a little crispier, I make my honey chicken, which has a light batter and a sweet honey garlic sauce. On nights when I want something nutty and savory, cashew chicken is on the table in about 30 minutes.

Do You Really Need the Bourbon?

Bourbon is listed as optional in this recipe, but I highly recommend adding it if you have some on hand. I tested this recipe with and without bourbon, and the difference was easy to taste. The bourbon adds a rich, slightly smoky flavor that balances nicely with the sweetness of the brown sugar and apple juice. It’s what gives bourbon chicken its name, after all!

The version without bourbon is still very flavorful. It’s a great option if you don’t have bourbon on hand or want to avoid cooking with alcohol. Just swap in an equal amount of apple juice or chicken broth.

Much of the alcohol cooks off during the 20-minute simmer, but a small amount can remain. If you’re cooking for anyone who avoids alcohol completely, leave it out. You don’t need anything fancy here, and any bourbon you would happily sip works great.

Tips for Beginners

  • Thicken the sauce with a cornstarch slurry. When I tested this recipe, the sauce was still quite thin when the 20-minute timer went off. If yours hasn’t thickened, stir in a slurry of 1 tablespoon cornstarch and 2 tablespoons cold water, and it will thicken right away. Keep the water to 2 tablespoons, because when I tested a full cup of water, the sauce got even more watery and took several extra minutes to start thickening.
  • Use the bourbon if you have it. The bourbon sauce is the star of this recipe, and much of the alcohol cooks down and evaporates as the cooking progresses. I tested it both ways, and the bourbon adds a rich, slightly smoky flavor that balances the sweet sauce. If you don’t have bourbon or want to skip it, you can swap it out with vegetable or chicken broth or apple juice, and the sauce is still very flavorful.
  • Add more veggies. I’m using ginger and garlic for flavor, but colorful bell peppers, bok choy, broccoli, zucchini, or cauliflower will also taste amazing. They soak up the sweet and spicy sauce and make this an easy one-pan meal.
  • Swap in chicken thighs. Boneless skinless chicken breast is my preferred cut because it’s lean protein, but boneless skinless chicken thighs also work. Dark meat takes a little longer, so give thighs a few extra minutes in the skillet to make sure they’re browned and cooked through.
  • Finish with a garnish. I like to top this dish with sesame seeds and sliced green onions right before serving. The green onions add a touch of freshness, and the sesame seeds add texture and a nutty flavor.

Bourbon Chicken Recipe

Bourbon Chicken is one of our favorite easy Chinese Takeout meals to make at home on busy nights. This Bourbon Chicken Recipe is the best sweet and spicy chicken recipe, with the best bourbon chicken sauce. Course Main Course Cuisine Chinese Difficulty Easy Prep Time 30 minutes minutes Cook Time 30 minutes minutes Total Time 1 hour hour Servings 6 servings Calories 301kcal Author Becky Hardin

Ingredients

  • 2 lbs. boneless chicken breasts cut into bite-size pieces
  • 2 tbsp olive oil
  • 1 garlic clove crushed or 1 tsp pre-minced garlic
  • ¼ tsp ginger
  • ½ tsp crushed red pepper flakes
  • ½ cup apple juice
  • ⅓ cup brown sugar
  • 2 tbsp ketchup
  • 1 tbsp cider vinegar
  • ½ cup water
  • ⅓ cup soy sauce or Yoshida’s Gourmet Sauce
  • 2 tbsp Bourbon optional but recommended
  • 1 tbsp cornstarch + 2 tbsp warm water optional for thickening

For Serving/Garnish

  • cooked white rice I use Minute Rice, prepared per instructions
  • sesame seeds optional, for garnish
  • sliced green onions optional, for garnish

Instructions

  • Heat oil in a large skillet over medium-low heat. Add chicken pieces and cook until lightly browned. 2 tbsp olive oil, 2 lbs. boneless chicken breasts
  • Once cooked, remove chicken.
  • Add remaining sauce ingredients, heating over medium heat until well mixed and dissolved. 1 garlic clove, ¼ tsp ginger, ½ tsp crushed red pepper flakes, ½ cup apple juice, ⅓ cup brown sugar, 2 tbsp ketchup, 1 tbsp cider vinegar, ½ cup water, ⅓ cup soy sauce or Yoshida’s Gourmet Sauce, 2 tbsp Bourbon
  • Add chicken and bring to a hard boil.
  • Reduce heat and simmer for 20 minutes or until thickened to the desired consistency.
  • (Optional thickening step) If the sauce is still thin, whisk the cornstarch and cold water together in a small bowl until smooth. Stir it into the simmering sauce and cook for about 1 minute, until thick and glossy 1 tbsp cornstarch + 2 tbsp warm water
  • Serve over hot rice. Garnish with sesame seeds and sliced green onions, if desired. cooked white rice, sesame seeds, sliced green onions

Video

Notes

  • Apple juice gives this sauce its fruity sweetness, but chicken broth or vegetable broth will do the trick if apple juice is not handy, so swap in an equal amount of broth. The sauce will taste a little more savory and a little less sweet.
  • The bourbon is optional, but it adds a rich, slightly smoky flavor that balances the sweetness of the sauce. If you don’t have it or want to avoid alcohol, replace it with an equal amount of apple juice or broth. The sauce will still be very flavorful.
  • If the sauce is still thin after 20 minutes of simmering, stir in a slurry of 1 tablespoon cornstarch and 2 tablespoons cold water. It thickens in about a minute. A larger amount of water thins the sauce out and takes much longer to cook down. You can also simmer for 3 to 5 extra minutes instead, but you’ll end up with less sauce and slightly tougher chicken.
  • Boneless skinless chicken breast is lean and cooks quickly, but chicken thighs also work. Since dark meat takes a bit longer, give thighs a few extra minutes to brown before you remove them from the skillet.
  • This sauce works with more than chicken. Swap the chicken for paneer or tofu for a vegetarian option. Pork and shrimp are other great options.
  • A simple garnish makes this dish feel finished, so sprinkle sesame seeds and sliced green onions over the top right before serving. The green onions add freshness and the sesame seeds add a nutty crunch.
  • Leftovers hold up well, so store them in an airtight container in the fridge for 3-4 days. Bourbon chicken also freezes for up to 3 months. The sauce may thin slightly after thawing, so simmer it for a few minutes while reheating to bring it back together.

Nutrition

Serving: 1serving | Calories: 301kcal | Carbohydrates: 18g | Protein: 34g | Fat: 9g | Saturated Fat: 2g | Polyunsaturated Fat: 1g | Monounsaturated Fat: 5g | Trans Fat: 0.02g | Cholesterol: 97mg | Sodium: 949mg | Potassium: 646mg | Fiber: 0.2g | Sugar: 15g | Vitamin A: 121IU | Vitamin C: 2mg | Calcium: 25mg | Iron: 1mg

How to Make Bourbon Chicken Step by Step

Gather all the ingredients together.

Prepare the chicken and cook: Cut 2 lbs of boneless chicken breasts into bite-sized pieces. Pat them dry. Heat 2 tbsp olive oil in a large skillet over medium-low heat. Add the chicken pieces and cook until lightly browned. Once cooked, remove the chicken. The chicken will change to a white color with a few light golden spots, and that’s when you need to remove it from the skillet. It doesn’t need to be cooked all the way through yet, since it will finish cooking in the sauce.

Make the bourbon sauce: Add 1 minced garlic clove, ¼ tsp ginger, ½ tsp crushed red pepper flakes, ½ cup apple juice, ⅓ cup brown sugar, 2 tbsp ketchup, 1 tbsp cider vinegar, ½ cup water, ⅓ cup soy sauce, and 2 tbsp bourbon to the same skillet. Heat over medium heat until well mixed and dissolved. The sauce is ready for the chicken when you no longer see any grains of brown sugar on the bottom of the pan.

Combine and simmer: Place the chicken back into the skillet and bring the mixture to a boil. Reduce the heat and let it simmer for 20 minutes, stirring now and then so the sugar doesn’t stick to the bottom of the pan.

Thicken the sauce (optional): After 20 minutes, check the sauce. If it is still thin and soupy, whisk 1 tbsp of cornstarch with 2 tbsp of cold water in a small bowl until smooth. Stir the slurry into the simmering sauce, and it will thicken right away, turning glossy and coating the back of a spoon. Use only 2 tbsp of water here. When I tested a larger amount of water, the sauce got even thinner and needed several more minutes on the stove before it started to thicken.

(If you’d rather skip the cornstarch, you can simmer the sauce for another 3-5 minutes until it caramelizes and thickens. To check, run a spatula across the bottom of the pan. If the sauce briefly parts and leaves a clear line before flowing back together, it’s ready. Just know that the extra time reduces the amount of sauce, and the chicken can turn a bit tough.)

Serve over rice: When the chicken is ready, serve it over rice. Sprinkle with sesame seeds and sliced green onions for a little crunch and freshness. Enjoy!

How to Store and Reheat

Transfer any leftovers to an airtight container and refrigerate for 3 to 4 days. The sauce will thicken as it chills.

Freezer

  • Freeze after cooking. Due to the simplicity of bourbon chicken, you can make it ahead of time and freeze it in freezer-friendly storage bags for up to 3 months. Thaw overnight in the refrigerator and reheat in a saucepan or microwave. The sauce may thin out slightly after freezing, so let it simmer for a few minutes while reheating.
  • Freeze before cooking. If you love those fresh flavors from the sauce, then feel free to freeze the chicken and sauce before combining. Once the chicken is cooked, let it cool and come to room temperature. Prepare the sauce separately, and let that cool as well. Transfer them to separate containers and freeze. Thaw overnight and cook per the instructions.

Reheating

To reheat, use a saucepan over medium-low heat or microwave in 30 second intervals. If the sauce has gotten too thick in the fridge, add a splash of water and stir until it loosens back up.

Make Ahead

Stir the sauce ingredients together and refrigerate them in a jar for up to 2 days. Cut the chicken ahead too and keep it in a separate container. When it’s time for dinner, brown the chicken and pour in the sauce, and you’ve skipped most of the prep.

Serving Suggestions

Asian recipes usually pair well with plain steamed rice, and that’s exactly how I serve bourbon chicken most nights. The rice soaks up every drop of that sweet, sticky sauce. When I’m short on time, I use Minute Rice, and when I have a little longer, I make my Instant Pot Basmati Rice because it comes out fluffy without any babysitting.

To round out the plate, I add a big skillet of stir fry vegetables. The crisp veggies balance the sweet sauce, and I can make them while the chicken simmers.

For a full takeout night at home, I start with egg rolls or crab rangoon bites as an appetizer, then indulge in this delicious chicken! Just keep in mind the crab rangoon has shellfish if anyone at the table has an allergy. When I’m feeding a bigger crowd, I swap the plain rice for chicken fried rice to stretch the meal a little further.

Takeout-Style Skillet Chicken Dinners

  • Teriyaki Chicken: This teriyaki chicken gets a quick soy marinade and a homemade sweet and salty sauce thickened with cornstarch. Fans of bourbon chicken will love the same sticky, glossy coating with a simpler flavor.
  • Orange Chicken: Crispy, cornstarch-coated chicken is tossed in a tangy sauce made with fresh orange juice and zest. It’s a great pick for anyone who wants a brighter, citrusy alternative to the deeper flavor of bourbon sauce.
  • Honey Chicken: Lightly battered chicken is pan-fried until golden, then coated in a honey, garlic, and ginger sauce. It delivers the sweetness of bourbon chicken with an extra crispy texture.
  • Cashew Chicken: Bites of chicken are seared with toasted cashews and green onions in a savory hoisin and soy sauce. It comes together in about 30 minutes and is a good fit for nights when a less sweet takeout dish sounds better.

Knowing how to roast vegetables well is a power move. It’s a single technique that can be deployed across an impressive range of vegetables turning them into platters of food that is simple, unfussy, and just plain good.

How To Roast Vegetables – The Basics

You can use this basic technique for a wide range of vegetables – potatoes, beets, broccoli, cauliflower, string beans, cabbage, artichoke hearts, asparagus, etc. Seriously, don’t overthink it. Once you get your roasting technique nailed down, you can fancify things with added spices, under-dressings, and the like. But to start, here’s what to know:

  • High heat is your friend. Somewhere in the range of 400°F – it will give you tender interiors and nice, golden exteriors. Go too hot and you’ll burn the edges before the insides have time to cook. Some recipes might go a bit higher, others a bit lower, but if I’m just doing a basic vegetable roast, I typically set the dial right around 400°F.
  • Don’t crowd the pan. Your vegetables are going to put off a good amount of moisture as they roast, and they like some space to crisp instead of steam.
  • Cut vegetables to a similar size. This way they will cook in the same amount of time.
  • Group vegetables by cooking time. Like with like  on the pan. You want to arrange your ingredients in zones based on estimated cooking time. For example, put asparagus which doesn’t take long to roast, on a small pan (or at the edge of a larger pan). Place ingredients that take longer to cook on another pan or in another zone, for example-potatoes or winter squash, That way you can remove them as they finish cooking.
  • Don’t skimp on the oil. You don’t have to go wild, but make sure everything gets a thin, even coat of olive oil before roasting.

Use this play-by-play: Heat oven to about 400°F. Toss each type of vegetable in a large bowl with a splash of olive oil and a good pinch of salt I often add a couple scoops of nutritional yeast in at this point but that’s entirely optional. Roast, tossing a couple times after things start browning, until vegetables are tender and have a good amount of golden color.

Favorite Roasted Vegetable Recipes

  1. Lemon Roasted Potatoes

    These lemon roasted potatoes are made extra-special with very little effort, thanks to crushed garlic, lemon juice, herbs, and olive oil. For when you want to level up your roasted potatoes, but aren’t game for anything too wild.

  2. Roasted Cauliflower with Tahini

    A special roasted cauliflower inspired by Sara Forte’s new cookbook. It’s wildly delicious – loaded with herbs, dates, nuts, roasted red onions and tossed with a tahini dressing.

  3. Roasted Delicata Squash

    One of the best ways to make roasted delicata squash, inspired by my friend Molly Watson. This recipe uses the pretty, scalloped-edged cross-cuts of the delicata squash, a few small potatoes, chopped kale, radishes, almonds and a bold miso harissa dressing.

  4. Roasted Broccoli

    The bonus part of this roasted broccoli is the creamy tahini under-dressing along with the scallion-peanut sprinkle. Inspired by a sheet pan recipe in Alison Roman’s Dining In, this broccoli is on repeat.

  5. Roasted Mushroom Sandwich

    An open-faced mushroom sandwich on good bread slathered along with a bit of garlic, salted ricotta, and some herbs or crushed kale chips.

  3. Roasted Tomato Salad

  4. Roasted Tomato Salsa

    The deep, caramelized flavors of roasted tomatoes and onions alongside the smokiness of the chipotles equals an incredibly good salsa.

Fall is baking season, and all those apple and pumpkin spice treats make me want to get in the kitchen, too! If you’re gluten-free, you deserve delicious, wholesome treats that everyone will happily gobble up. That’s why I’ve rounded up 20 of my foolproof, crowd-pleasing gluten-free fall desserts made with real food ingredients. I test the heck out of my dessert recipes, so you can count on them to turn out right and taste amazing!

These treats aren’t too sweet, either, which is the highest praise my picky Asian mom can give a dessert. In fact, she insists that I make my pumpkin bars with dairy-free cream cheese frosting as soon as fall rolls around!

Don’t feel like turning on the oven? I’ve got you covered with my quick microwave apple and pumpkin mug cakes and my no-bake chocolate tart. You don’t have to spend an afternoon baking to enjoy a homemade treat!

One tip before you start: for most of my baking recipes, I highly recommend using a kitchen scale to measure your dry ingredients. A little extra flour can make a big difference, especially in gluten-free baking! Just click the Metric button in the recipe card to get the weights in grams.

Wanna know my 3 faves from this list?

  • Pumpkin Pie Bars: Pumpkin pie in bar form. Can it get any better than this?
  • Apple Mug Cake: In just about 2 minutes, you get the fluffiest and tastiest apple cake in a single serving size!
  • Apple Muffins with Paleo Streusel Topping: Wowie. These tender apple muffins are fab, but the crunchy, sweet, and slightly salty topping takes the cake! (Sorry for the pun…)

These Are What I’ve Been Eating For Breakfast Every Day

YUM these little meal prep apple muffins have been such a treat for my mornings!

Open windows! Crisp fall air! Hot coffee! A warm, streusely, lightly cinnamony apple muffin with tons of wholesome stuff in it and a generous pat of butter on top! It’s what I’m doing every day right now, and it’s my dream life.

The main ingredients here are shockingly basic:

  • oats
  • apples
  • eggs
  • yogurt
  • vanilla
  • cinnamon

I threw some brown sugar streusel on top, because streusel increases my interest in any muffin by about 500%.

I’ve been packing one of these in my girls’ lunchboxes almost every time we pack a cold lunch! I also bring them for a car snack after school pick up. My kids honestly eat one of these (or these pumpkin ones, or these zucchini ones) once a day at this point.

Hope you love them!

Just Some Things I Tried and Have Opinions About

I don’t know that anyone cares about this, but I spend too much time thinking about these things.

  • This recipe works best with Greek yogurt rather than regular yogurt! It gets more evenly brown and has a nice rise like you see in the pictures, whereas regular yogurt is wetter and makes the batter more dense. (It does still work, if you’re in a pinch.)
  • You want rolled old fashioned oats for this one – don’t use the Costco sprouted oats, and do not use quick oats. They both absorb liquid in a different way. I just buy the Quaker old fashioned rolled oats!
  • I did try this with grating the apples, but I prefer just throwing them into the blender at the end of blending. 1) It’s easier, and 2) I felt like the muffins got a better rise with the apples being more incorporated.
  • Our previous muffin recipes similar to this have used maple syrup, but it gets really expensive to use that much real maple syrup, so I switched to brown sugar for this one!
  • The streusel is optional – they’re still pretty and delicious without!
  • The batter will bake differently the longer it sits! I find he sweet spot is about 10 minutes. You may find that your second batch gets a bit more puffy than your first.
  • I typically fill the muffins 2/3 of the way full so they rise up nicely but don’t completely spill over the top. Like so:

Thanks for coming to my Ted Talk! 🙂

Apple Streusel Meal Prep Muffins

5 Stars 4 Stars 3 Stars 2 Stars 1 Star

4.8 from 5 reviews

  • Author: Lindsay
  • Total Time: 33 minutes
  • Yield: 16–18 muffins 1x
Print Pin

Description

These apple streusel meal prep muffins are super wholesome, loaded with streusel, and ideal for prepping ahead! My kids love them!

Ingredients

Units USM

For the Muffins:

  • 1 cup whole milk Greek yogurt
  • 3 eggs
  • 3 1/2 cups old-fashioned oats (I don’t recommend the Costco sprouted oats or quick oats)
  • 3/4 cup brown sugar, lightly packed
  • 1 1/2 teaspoons baking soda
  • 1 teaspoon baking powder
  • 1 teaspoon vanilla
  • 1/2 teaspoon cinnamon
  • 1/2 teaspoon kosher salt
  • 2 cups roughly chopped Honeycrisp apples (I did not peel mine)

For the Streusel:

  • 4 tablespoons butter, melted
  • 1/3 cup brown sugar
  • 1/2 cup + 3 tablespoons all-purpose flour
Cook Mode Prevent your screen from going dark

Instructions

  1. Prep: Preheat the oven to 350 degrees. Spray a muffin tin with avocado oil.
  2. Blend: Add ingredients to the blender in order, but leave out the apples for now. Blend in short bursts until it starts to move (you may need to help it along initially). Once it gets going, blend for 15-20 seconds, until mostly smooth. There should still be some flecks of texture, similar to a bran muffin batter.
  3. Apples: Add the apple chunks; blend again for 15-20 seconds until they are worked into the batter in small flecks.
  4. Streusel: Mix the streusel ingredients in a small bowl until it resembles small pebbles – I start with a fork, and then mix with my hands. (The batter can rest for 10 minutes during this time.)
  5. Assemble: Pour batter in the muffin tin – filling each cup 2/3 of the way full, but not all the way to the top. Add a spoonful of streusel on each one. (I typically get 16-17 muffins from this recipe, so I bake in two batches.)
  6. Bake: Bake for 16-18 minutes, until the tops spring back when gently pressed. Cool for 10-15 minutes so they are easier to remove from the pan. I love eating these warm with a shmear of butter!
  7. Storing: I put them in a ziplock bag and keep them in the freezer or fridge. To reheat, I pop them in the microwave for 30 seconds (from fridge) or 60 seconds (from frozen).

Equipment

Blender

Muffin Pan

📅 Oct 08, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Oh, my friends. I am very excited to share this with you today. I dreamed up this quesadilla many years ago and finally got around to making it and taking photos of it last fall. And let me tell you, it fulfills every food fantasy I had and then some.

I made this quesadilla for lunch the day after Thanksgiving. And then I made it again for dinner for myself and for the rest of the family too. And then? I made it again on Saturday AND on Sunday. Yep, I did that. And I will happily be making this quesadilla again, on repeat, for as long as the Thanksgiving sides last this year.

I have made a lot of quesadillas. But, I am telling you that this Thanksgiving quesadilla is officially my favorite way to use the Thanksgiving leftovers, alongside my more traditional turkey quesadilla when those leftovers finally start running out.

Thanksgiving Quesadilla

From start to finish, this quesadilla only takes ten minutes to make. It’s built from leftovers, so it’s easy to adapt on the fly. So, don’t worry if you don’t have all the ingredients. Just use what you have.

I don’t even care if I sound crazy. I’ve been raving to all of my brothers and sisters, friends and strangers at the grocery store even, about this way to use up leftovers ever since I first landed on it, almost two years ago. A little bit of this and a little bit of that add up to whole lot of deliciousness!

Ingredients and Substitutions

Tortillas – I prefer the flavor and texture of flour tortillas, hands down. But, you can go with corn tortillas if you want. (If you choose to use corn tortillas, you’ll need two and you won’t be able to fold them as described below.)

Cheese – Monterey jack cheese is my go-to here, but whatever mild, melty cheese you have on hand will work well.

Butter – I use salted butter in all of my recipes, unless otherwise stated. It helps the tortilla brown and keep from sticking to the pan.

Turkey – Whatever leftover turkey you have on hand post-Thanksgiving will work. I always use our roast turkey. If you happen to run short of turkey, you can sub rotisserie chicken here to make the most of any remaining side dishes.

Green Bean Casserole – My favorite green bean casserole brings wonderful flavors and texture to the mix.

Stuffing – While it lasts (which isn’t long) I include some of this sausage apple cranberry stuffing. Any stuffing you have will work.

Creamed Corn – I love adding creamed corn (or even corn pudding if we’re lucky enough to have any leftover!) to the quesadilla for the sweet crisp bite the kernels add.

Mashed Potatoes – Mashed potatoes add some creamy, rich flavor and support .

Sweet Potatoes – If you have them, don’t sleep on adding sweet potatoes as well! They add a great, mildly sweet contrast to the other savory ingredients.

Cranberry Sauce – My boys and I love to use some leftover cranberry sauce for dipping. Don’t judge me til you’ve tried it.

Notes on Equipment

  • Large nonstick skillet. I have been through about a million skillets over the years. For the past several years this has been my everyday champ, and it crisps a fully loaded quesadilla without a thing sticking.
  • Grater
  • Flexible nylon turner. I reach for this turner almost every single day. It’s thin enough to slide cleanly under the folded quesadilla yet sturdy enough to flip it without losing the filling, and the nylon is gentle on my nonstick pan.
  • Sharp knife
  • Cutting board

How to Make a Thanksgiving Quesadilla

Gathering the Ingredients: Before I start cooking, I gather all of the ingredients and place them next to the stove for easy assembly.

Preheating the Pan: Set a large non-stick pan over medium-low heat on the stove to warm up.

Prepping the Ingredients: While it heats, you’ll want to shred the cheese and chop the turkey and green bean casserole into tiny pieces.

Buttering the Tortilla: I lightly butter one side of a tortilla. Once the pan is hot, I set it buttered side down in the pan.

Adding Cheese and Turkey: Sprinkle half of the tortilla with about half the cheese, then layer the chopped turkey on top of the cheese.

Topping with the Rest: You’ll want to layer very small amounts of the green bean casserole, stuffing, mashed potatoes, and sweet potatoes over the chicken. I use my hands to place little pieces of each dish all over half of the tortilla. Once I have all the layers in place, I add a final layer of cheese.

Folding It Over: Now you’ll gently fold the side of the tortilla over the fillings and press down with the back of a spatula.

Cooking: You’ll let it cook undisturbed for 2-3 minutes, until the cheese begins to melt and the bottom tortilla is a golden brown.

Flipping: I carefully flip the folded quesadilla over to the other side and let it continue cooking for another 2-3 minutes.

Serving: Once both sides are nice and crispy, use your spatula to transfer the quesadilla to a cutting board and slice into wedges while still hot. Serve warm with cranberry sauce.

Expert Tip

This is a really hearty quesadilla. Even if you’re only adding a little bit of each ingredient, it all adds up. And, when you’re working with a thick quesadilla like this, heat management plays a big role. While I normally cook my quesadillas over medium heat, I cook this one a little slower to allow all of the ingredients time to warm and grow toasty while the cheese melts. If you’d like to cook them faster, I recommend heating the leftovers in the microwave for 30 seconds or so before cooking the quesadillas.

Use Your Hands – I find it simplest to just use my hands to distribute small portions of potatoes and green beans and stuffing across the quesadilla. Yes, it’s messy, but the results are worth it and you’ll have plenty of time to wash up while the quesadilla is getting toasty in the pan. Also, disposable gloves are a beautiful thing.

Chopping Filling Ingredients Finely – Chop those ingredients smaller than you think. The pieces of turkey and green beans need to be tiny and bite-size, or it’s going to be messy trying to eat it. Aim for ½” pieces.

Keeping Layers Thin – While it might be tempting to pack as much as possible into these quesadillas, patience is key. Cramming too much filling into these quesadillas will prevent them from cooking evenly, and the tortilla may not be able to keep everything contained.

Serving Suggestions

The one thing I never skip is a little dish of cranberry on the side for dipping. homemade cranberry sauce is the classic move, but hot pepper cranberry sauce brings a little heat that plays well against all that savory filling. A spoonful of cranberry chutney or cranberry pepper jelly is good here too, if you have a jar in the refrigerator.

Make Ahead & Storage

How to Store: Leftover quesadilla wedges can be stored in an airtight container in the fridge for 3 days.

How to Reheat: I usually reheat the quesadilla in a nonstick skillet on medium heat, to refresh the crispiness. You can also reheat in the air fryer.

More Quesadilla Recipes

  • Teriyaki Chicken Quesadillas
  • Carnitas Quesadillas
  • Breakfast Quesadillas
  • Street Corn Quesadillas
  • Ham and Cheese Quesadillas
  • Chicken Bacon Ranch Quesadillas
  • Korean Beef Quesadillas

Frequently Asked Questions

What is the best way to flip a quesadilla without spilling?

I usually use a large spatula to circle the base of the pan, checking for any areas where the tortilla might be stuck. After doing that, I slip the spatula underneath the quesadilla in one quick, smooth motion. Then, I lift the quesadilla just enough to be able to flip it over on the folded edge, keeping the tortilla within the bounds of the pan.

Should I strain/drain any of the filling ingredients?

I typically don’t need to. But, if something has a lot of juices, you’re right to be concerned. Too much moisture is a bad thing. Use a slotted spoon and leave the juice behind.

How do I slice a quesadilla without making a mess?

One of the best things I’ve learned is to wait about a minute and let it rest before slicing. The cheese is still plenty hot, but it binds the other ingredients better.

Print

Thanksgiving Quesadilla

This Thanksgiving quesadilla layers leftover turkey, stuffing, corn, mashed potatoes, and more into a crispy tortilla. It's the best possible use for the leftovers. Course Main Course Cuisine Mexican Prep Time 10 minutes minutes Cook Time 5 minutes minutes Total Time 10 minutes minutes Servings 1 serving Calories 509kcal Author Mary Younkin

Ingredients

  • 1 teaspoon butter
  • 1 8-10" flour tortilla
  • ⅓ cup freshly shredded Monterey jack cheese
  • ¼ cup Roast Turkey chopped into ½-inch pieces
  • 2-3 tablespoons Green Bean Casserole roughly chopped into ½-inch pieces
  • 2-3 tablespoons Sausage Apple Cranberry Stuffing or your favorite stuffing recipe
  • 2 tablespoons Creamed Corn
  • 2 tablespoons Herb Mashed Potatoes
  • 2 tablespoons Whipped Sweet Potatoes
  • Cranberry Sauce for serving

Instructions

  • Place all of the quesadilla fillings next to the stove for easy assembly. Warm a nonstick skillet over medium-low heat. Lightly butter one side of the tortilla with butter and place it in the hot pan. Generously sprinkle cheese over the tortilla and top half of one side with turkey.
  • Working quickly, top the turkey with green bean casserole, stuffing, mashed potatoes, and sweet potatoes. Fold the cheese side of the tortilla over the fillings and press it down over them. Cover with a lid.
  • Let the quesadilla cook for 2-3 minutes. When the cheese is beginning to melt and the bottom tortilla is browning slightly, uncover and flip the folded quesadilla over to cook the other side.
  • Continue to cook uncovered until all the cheese is melted and the tortilla is crisp around the edges. Using a spatula, transfer the quesadilla back to a cutting board and cut it into wedges while it is hot and serve warm with cranberry sauce.

📅 Oct 07, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

This pumpkin sushi is made with sweet, crispy crusted pieces of winter squash tucked inside rolls of nori and rice, then finished with a tangy vegan "eel" sauce. It's a fun, decadent homemade sushi roll that's perfect for fall!

I love vegan sushi, but when I eat sushi, I eat serious sushi. Yes, basic veggie sushi is nice, but, especially if I'm going to the trouble of making homemade sushi, I want something really special. I'm talking decadence! Think peanut avocado sushi, cream cheese veggie sushi, or smoked tofu sushi. These rolls will not leave you hungry, and OMG are they tasty.

[feast_advanced_jump_to]

But my favorite sushi roll to order at a restaurant has always been tempura pumpkin sushi. Have you had it? These rolls are stuffed with sweet winter squash covered in crispy tempura batter and served with eel sauce (which is often accidentally vegan!). It's about as delicious and satisfying as sushi gets, and I had to come up with a recipe so I could make it at home.

This vegan pumpkin sushi works with most types of sweet winter squash, so it's perfect for the cooler months of the year. You'll be dipping strips of the squash in tempura batter (adapted from my vegetable tempura recipe), then frying them until golden and crispy, and wrapping it all in sushi rice and nori. It's exactly what you need on those occasions when you're craving sushi and comfort food.

Ingredients You'll Need

Below you'll find a list of ingredients in this recipe, with notes and substitutions. Scroll all the way to the bottom of the post to see the full recipe, including the amount of each ingredient.

  • Sushi rice. Stick with sushi rice rather than any old variety of rice for this recipe. It's super sticky and has a short grain, so it's perfect for shaping and holding together sushi rolls.
  • Rice vinegar. You can find this in the international section of the grocery store.
  • Salt.
  • Sugar. Use organic sugar to keep the recipe vegan. Conventional sugar may be processed with animal bone char.
  • Soy sauce. Tamari and liquid aminos will both work as substitutes.
  • Brown sugar. This should also be organic.
  • Mirin. This is a cooking wine you can find in the international section of most grocery stores.
  • Cornstarch.
  • Toasted sesame oil. This is a flavoring oil — not a cooking oil. Look for it in the international aisle.
  • Vegetable oil. Any high-heat oil you normally use for frying is good here — peanut oil, corn oil, and canola oil are all fine options.
  • Winter squash. I generally use butternut squash for my pumpkin rolls, but acorn, kabocha, and delicata are also great options. Sugar pumpkin will work, but it won't be as sweet or dense as the other options.
  • Flour. I've only tested the recipe with all-purpose wheat flour (also known as white flour), so that's your best bet for success!
  • Beer or seltzer water. I love making my tempura with beer, which adds a savory, slightly bitter flavor. But seltzer water is totally fine if that's what you've got.
  • Baking powder. You only need this if you're going with seltzer water.
  • Nori.
  • Accompaniments. You'll want to grab some toasted sesame seeds, pickled ginger, and wasabi paste from the international aisle at the store.

How It's Made

Below is a detailed photo tutorial on how to make this dish. Scroll all the way down if you'd like to skip right to the recipe!

Step 1: Soak the rice. Place it in a saucepan and cover it with water. Let it sit for about 20 minutes, then drain and rinse it. Return it to the pot.

Step 2: Cook the rice. Add water to the pot, along with rice vinegar, salt, and sugar. Place it over high heat, get the water boiling, then reduce the heat and let it simmer until the water is absorbed. Leave the lid on the pot for about 10 minutes when it's done, then fluff it with a fork.

Steps 3 & 4: Make the sauce. Combine your soy sauce, brown sugar, and mirin in a small pot, place it over medium heat, and bring it to a simmer. Let it cook at a low simmer for five minutes, while you mix some cold water and cornstarch. Add the mixture to the sauce, let it thicken, then take it off of heat and add the sesame oil.

Step 5: Heat your oil. Place about 2 inches of oil into a large pot, and set it over medium heat. Let it heat up while you begin preparing the fillings. You'll want it to get to about 350°F.

Step 6: Precook the squash. Cut the squash into small baton-shaped pieces, arrange them on a microwave-safe plate, cover it with a damp paper towel, then zap them for about 3 minutes. You just want to start softening them up.

Steps 7 & 8: Prepare dipping stations. Place some flour in one bowl, and in a second bowl, whisk together flour, cornstarch, salt, and either ale or seltzer water. If you go with seltzer water, you'll also need some baking powder.

Step 9: Fry the squash. Start by dredging a few pieces in the flour you just added to the bowl, then dip them in the batter, and transfer them to the hot oil. Fry your squash in batches, being careful not to crowd the pot. Once they're golden brown, place them on a wire rack to drain.

Steps 10 & 11: Prepare to roll. Cover your bamboo mat with plastic, then place a sheet of nori over the mat. Spread rice in a thin layer on the nori, wetting your hands to keep the rice from sticking.

Steps 12-16: Fill and roll. Flip your rice covered nori, then arrange the squash in a strip, about an inch from the edge. Roll it up tightly. Slice it into 8 pieces, then repeat the process to make 3 more rolls.

Tip: This short video demonstrates the process for rolling uramaki, also known as an inside out sushi roll. If you're not feeling that today, try making this recipe as maki (nori on the outside), which you can see demonstrated in this video.

Step 17: Finish and serve. Sprinkle your rolls with sesame seeds, then serve them with your vegan eel sauce, pickled ginger, and wasabi.

Variations

  • Add to the filling. Tempura-fried squash goes great with chives, avocado, vegan cream cheese, pickled carrots, daikon, or cucumber. Or get creative and try your own additions!
  • Crunchy topping. Sprinkle the finished rolls with crushed tempura bits or fried onions for some extra crunch.
  • Quinoa sushi. Replace up to half the sushi rice with cooked quinoa.

Frequently Asked Questions

Can this recipe be made gluten-free?

I haven't tried making this recipe with gluten-free flour, so I'm not sure. If you'd like to try it, I think an all-purpose gluten-free flour blend would be your best bet. You'll also need to make sure your beer is gluten-free (or stick with seltzer), and replace the soy sauce with gluten-free tamari.

Can I make this recipe without frying?

Yes, but I recommend skipping the tempura if you'd like to bake it. Simply roast the squash instead. You can follow the basic method in my roasted sweet potato sushi recipe.

Can I use brown rice?

Yes, but make sure it's brown sushi rice. You may need to alter the cooking method a bit — refer to the package instructions.

How should I store leftover pumpkin sushi?

Pumpkin sushi is best served the day it's made, but if you do have leftovers, they'll keep in an airtight container in the fridge for up to three days.

What to Serve with Pumpkin Sushi

  • Japanese Salad with Carrot Ginger Dressing
  • Hibachi-Style Japanese Fried Rice with Smoky Baked Tofu
  • Comforting Vegan Miso Soup
  • Crispy Baked Teriyaki Tofu

Tempura Pumpkin Sushi

This pumpkin sushi is made with sweet, crispy crusted pieces of winter squash tucked inside rolls of nori and rice, then finished with a tangy vegan "eel" sauce. It's a fun, decadent homemade sushi roll that's perfect for fall! Course Entree Cuisine American, Japanese Prep Time 20 minutes minutes Cook Time 35 minutes minutes Soak Time 20 minutes minutes Total Time 1 hour hour 15 minutes minutes Servings 4 Calories 652kcal Author Alissa Saenz

Equipment

  • 1 Sushi mat
  • 1 Instant read thermometer

Ingredients

For the Rice

  • 1 ⅓ cups sushi rice
  • 1 ½ cups water, plus more for soaking
  • 2 tablespoons rice vinegar
  • 1 ½ teaspoons salt
  • 1 teaspoon organic granulated sugar

For the Vegan Eel Sauce

  • ¼ cup soy sauce
  • ¼ cup organic brown sugar
  • ¼ cup mirin
  • 1 ½ tablespoons cold water
  • 2 teaspoons cornstarch
  • 1 teaspoon toasted sesame oil

For the Filling

  • Vegetable oil
  • 12 ounces winter squash (such as butternut, kabocha, delicata, or acorn), cut into ¼ to ½ inch thick batons
  • ½ cup all-purpose flour

For the Tempura Batter

  • ½ cup all-purpose flour
  • ¼ cup cornstarch
  • ½ teaspoon salt
  • ¼ teaspoon baking powder (only use if you're making the batter with seltzer — do not use with beer)
  • ¾ cup vegan ale or plain seltzer water, chilled

For Rolling

  • 4 nori sheets

For Serving

  • Toasted sesame seeds
  • Pickled ginger
  • Wasabi paste
US Customary - Metric

Instructions

  • Place the rice into a medium saucepan and cover it with cold water. Let it sit for 20 minutes, then drain the water and return the rice to the pot.
  • Add the 1 ½ cups of water, rice vinegar, salt, and sugar. Stir, then set the pot over medium heat. Bring the water to a boil, cover the pot, and reduce the heat to a low simmer. Let the rice simmer for about 20 minutes, until the liquid is all absorbed. Turn off the heat and let the pot sit covered for 10 minutes. Uncover the pot and fluff the rice with a fork.
  • Make the sauce while the rice cooks. Stir the soy sauce, brown sugar, and mirin together in a small saucepan. Set it over medium heat and bring the mixture to a simmer. Reduce the heat and let it cook at a low simmer for about 5 minutes.
  • While the mixture simmers, stir the cold water and cornstarch together in a small container. After the sauce has simmered for 5 minutes, stir in the cornstarch mixture and bring the mixture back to a simmer. Once it starts to simmer, it should thicken slightly. Remove it from the heat, then stir in the sesame oil. Let the sauce cool.
  • Place about 2 inches of oil into a large pot and set it over medium heat.
  • While the oil heats up, place the squash batons onto a microwave-safe plate and cover them with a damp paper towel. Microwave the squash for about 3 minutes, until the pieces are just beginning to soften up. Be careful when removing the paper towel, as the squash will release hot steam.
  • Place the ½ cup of flour into a small bowl.
  • In a second bowl, whisk together the batter ingredients.
  • Once the oil reaches about 350°F, place a few of the squash batons into the bowl containing the flour. Gently roll the batons to coat them in flour, then, one at a time, dip each baton into the batter. Coat the baton with batter, then shake off any excess before transferring it to the hot oil. Repeat the process, adding as many batons as you can at a time without crowding (you'll end up with about 4 batches). Fry the batons for about 3 minutes, then use a slotted spoon to transfer them to a wire rack to drain. Continue until all of the squash is fried.
  • Wrap a bamboo mat in plastic wrap and place one sheet of nori over top. Keep a small bowl of water nearby and wet your fingertips.
  • Distribute one quarter of your rice in an even layer over the nori, pressing it down until it's nice and thin. Wet your hands periodically to help prevent the rice from sticking to them.
  • Carefully invert your rice-covered nori sheet so that the rice layer sits on the plastic wrap and the nori is face up.
  • Arrange about a quarter of your squash filling in a strip extending widthwise across your nori, about an inch away from you.
  • Carefully roll the end of the mat closest to you over your filling strip, tucking and squeezing to get it nice and tight. Continue rolling until you reach the end.
  • Take your roll and place it seam side down on a cutting board. Using a large, sharp knife, cut the roll into eight pieces, wiping the blade clean with a damp towel between cuts.
  • Repeat the process to make three more rolls using the remaining ingredients.
  • Sprinkle the rolls with toasted sesame seeds, then serve with vegan eel sauce, pickled ginger, and wasabi.

Notes

Watch this quick video if you need guidance on how to roll uramaki.

Nutrition

📅 Oct 05, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Creamy green beans and mushrooms are nestled beneath crispy onions in this Fresh Green Bean Casserole. If you’ve been looking for “the one” new recipe to make this holiday season, look no further. I may be biased, but it doesn’t get any better than this!

My friends, there is no bigger meal at our house than Thanksgiving. Each year, it gives me an opportunity to re-make and tweak all of our favorite recipes. And this fresh green bean casserole recipe is one improvement that I’m particularly proud of. This year, it will find a home on the table between my versions of mom’s corn pudding casserole and Aunt Judy’s strawberry pretzel salad.

Fresh Green Bean Casserole

At our house, we have a tradition with Thanksgiving. All of us get to pick one “favorite” dish for the lineup, and I promise to make everything on that list. And, at this point, my love of checklists and systems has really nailed down the process of how to make Thanksgiving dinner stress-free.

But, here’s the secret… it doesn’t matter what the favorites are. They want them all. If something gets overlooked, there’s a mutiny in the kitchen that starts with a chorus of “But, we have to have that!”

Believe me, I’m not complaining. I love knowing that my family loves eating these dishes. Thanksgiving is a holiday that I remember fondly from my childhood. There were times that we struggled, but my mom never failed to make this opportunity to practice gratitude feel like a feast.

You probably already know your way around a green been casserole. But, I’m convinced that this version is my best one yet. It starts with fresh beans, giving it the texture upgrade that keeps them tender crisp instead of becoming mushy.

It’s the classic side dish you already know and love, but with a little more life in every bite. A little more effort? Sure. But it’s so, so worth it.

Ingredients and Substitutions

The Beans – I’ll give my full advice on how to buy the best green beans in the Expert Tip, but be choosy with your beans. This is the ingredient that makes or breaks this casserole.

Butter & Garlic – I use salted butter and fresh garlic when cooking the mushrooms. It makes a difference, but you can swap for garlic powder in a pinch.

Mushrooms – You can use whatever fresh mushrooms you have on hand, but my favorite varieties are white button and cremini. They have a rich, savory flavor, and are easy to prepare.

Seasonings – I use kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper in my cooking, unless otherwise specified.

The Sauce – We’re going to use a mix of chicken broth and heavy cream to make the cream sauce. You could swap for a lower-fat dairy option, but I don’t recommend it. The heavy cream makes this dish taste richer.

Flour – This is used to thicken the sauce. Any all-purpose flour will do.

The Topping – Fried onions are the classic finish for this casserole. And, with good reason! I find that it’s worth it to splurge on the French’s brand for this dish. I’ve tried a few store brands, and the texture isn’t the same.

Notes on Equipment

  • Chef’s knife. Food prep is so much easier when you have a quality knife, and there’s a lot to prep for Thanksgiving! I first tried these knives 10 years ago, while visiting a professional test kitchen. You could say it was love at first slice. That’s why mine has been in my kitchen ever since.
  • Cutting board
  • Large pot
  • Wooden spoon
  • Colander
  • Large bowl
  • Measuring cups and spoons
  • 9×13 Baking dish. I adore Pyrex dishes. They’re one of those kitchen staples that that just makes cooking easier. And, I can vouch for how sturdy and durable they are (I’ve had some of mine almost 20 years). And, who doesn’t love a dish that comes with a tight-fitting lid?

How to Make Fresh Green Bean Casserole

Getting Stuff Started: I start by setting a pot of water over high heat on the stove. Then, I preheat the oven to 350°F.

Prepping the Vegetables: While the water heats up, we’ll rinse the green beans. Then, you just have to trim the ends and chop them into 2-inch pieces. I clean the mushrooms and then chop them into ½-inch pieces, and mince the garlic while I’m at it.

Boiling the Beans: Once the water is boiling, I add the green beans and salt. You’ll let them cook for 5-6 minutes until tender crisp.

Setting Up the Ice: While the beans are cooking, we’re going to fill a large bowl with ice water and set it up next to the sink.

Chilling the Beans: Drain the beans in a colander and plunge them immediately into the ice water to stop cooking. Once cool, I drain them again.

Cooking the Mushrooms: Don’t wash that pot! Instead, we’re going to melt butter over medium-high heat in it. Then, I add the mushrooms, garlic, salt, and pepper. You need to cook them about 5 minutes, until all the liquid has been released, reabsorbed, and evaporated.

Adding Flour: Sprinkle the flour over the mushrooms and cook for about a minute, stirring constantly.

Making the Sauce: I stir in the chicken broth next and keep stirring until it comes to a simmer. Then, reduce the heat to medium and add your heavy cream.

Simmering: Continue stirring and simmer for 2 to 3 minutes, until the sauce thickens. Give it a taste and add salt and pepper if desired.

Assembling the Casserole: I place the green beans in a 9×13 baking dish and pour the sauce over them. Stir carefully so everything is evenly coated.

Baking: Top the casserole with a layer of fried onions, and then pop it into the oven for 15-20 minutes. You’ll know it’s ready when the sauce is hot and bubbling.

Expert Tip

The secret to the best green bean casserole should probably come as no surprise. It’s all about the texture. When you blanch the beans, you don’t want to overcook them. We’re just giving them the head start they need before going into the oven. The goal is for them to come out of the oven mostly soft with just the slightest bite remaining. You don’t want a soft, mushy filling beneath a crunchy topping. We’re going to serve a creamy sauce, soft mushrooms, slightly crisp green beans, and a crunchy topping. Together, that balance of textures takes it right over the top.

Taste Before Baking – This is the pro move that chefs make. So, don’t skip the taste test. Make sure that before you load up the baking dish, you’re happy with the balance of flavors in the cream sauce. Adjust salt and pepper to taste!

Make It Ahead – I used to juggle all the things on the stove and in the oven on the big day. But, you can actually make the sauce and blanch the green beans ahead of time, and store them separately a few days in advance. That makes it super easy to assemble the casserole and pop it into the oven right before the meal.

Let It Rest – There are two good reasons why you don’t want to serve this one immediately. First, that’s a recipe for getting burned. Nobody likes that! But, on another level, you need to give it some time to cool so that the sauce can thicken.

Serving Suggestions

I’m not the boss of you, ok? But, if you’re making this fresh green bean casserole and aren’t plating it next to my sausage apple cranberry stuffing, then you are missing out. That creamy sauce is going to run between the stuffing and a big spoonful of whipped sweet potatoes at my house, and I am not one bit sorry about it.

And, if you’re still working on the main event, let me show you how we roast the best turkey in just one hour! Because if you don’t make these green beans along with all the rest, you won’t have all the leftovers you’ll need for the ultimate Thanksgiving quesadilla.

Make Ahead & Storage

Make Ahead: For a regular Sunday dinner, I just make this as part of dinner. But, when it comes to holiday cooking, I start prepping this a couple of days in advance, storing the sauce and beans separately. Then, you can just assemble it when ready to cook.

How to Store: You can store leftovers in the fridge for up to 3 days, or save it in the freezer for up to a month.

How to Reheat: I reheat each serving in the microwave, in 30 to 60 second increments. If you’re reheating a larger batch, you may want to warm it over medium-low heat on the stove. You can refresh the sauce with a splash of milk, if it has thickened. And, don’t skip adding another sprinkle of fried onions to give it some crunch.

More Holiday Side Dish Recipes

  • Rudy’s Slow-Cooker Creamed Corn
  • Hot Pepper Cranberry Sauce
  • Rustic Mashed Potatoes
  • Perfect Brown Gravy (Made Without Meat Drippings)
  • Corn Pudding Casserole

Frequently Asked Questions

How can I make green bean casserole ahead of time?

I typically prep this recipe a few days before I plan to serve it. I wrap the boiled and chilled green beans in a paper towel, then place them in a gallon-size freezer bag in the refrigerator. Store the cooked and cooled sauce in an airtight container beside the beans. When you’re ready to make the casserole, just follow the directions below to assemble and cook it in the oven.

Can I use a slow cooker for this one?

Absolutely! I’m a huge believer in using slow cookers on Thanksgiving to ensure everything stays hot and finishes at the same time. Just combine the green beans and the sauce in a 6 qt slow cooker and set it to HIGH until bubbling, then reduce to LOW for another 2-3 hours. Just before serving, remove the lid and top with fried onions.

Side note: I love that Crock-pot for transporting foods because it has a locking lid. You can also use a 3 quart version, but I prefer the larger one since it gives me more real estate for those tasty fried onions.

What kind of green beans should I use?

For this recipe, I find that regular green beans are ideal. The thickness makes them a little less finicky when it comes to achieving the ideal texture when they come out of the oven. You can use haricots verts for visual interest, as they’re thinner, but it’s far harder to achieve the slightly crisp texture that makes this casserole stand out.

Print

Fresh Green Bean Casserole

Skip the canned soup! This Fresh Green Bean Casserole has a from-scratch mushroom cream sauce, and crispy fried onions on top. Course Side Dish Cuisine American Prep Time 15 minutes minutes Cook Time 30 minutes minutes Total Time 45 minutes minutes Servings 10 servings Calories 260kcal Author Mary Younkin

Ingredients

  • 2 lbs fresh green beans ends trimmed and chopped into 2-inch pieces
  • 2 tablespoons kosher salt for cooking
  • 3 tablespoons butter
  • 1 lb fresh mushrooms chopped into ½-inch pieces
  • 4 cloves garlic minced
  • ½ teaspoon kosher salt
  • ¼ teaspoon freshly ground black pepper
  • ¼ cup all-purpose flour
  • 1½ cups chicken broth
  • 1½ cups heavy cream
  • 3-6 ounces canned fried onions for topping

Instructions

Blanching Instructions

  • Bring a large pot of water to a boil. Add green beans and 2 tablespoons salt to boiling water. Cook beans until bright green and crisp-tender, about 5-6 minutes.
  • Fill a large bowl with ice water while beans are cooking. Set it next to the sink. Drain beans in a colander and plunge immediately into ice water to stop cooking. Once cooled, drain beans again in the colander.

Sauce Instructions

  • Add butter to the empty pot and melt over medium-high heat until foaming subsides. Add mushrooms, garlic, salt, and pepper; cook until mushrooms are tender and all liquid evaporates, about 5 minutes.
  • Sprinkle mushrooms with flour and cook for 1 minute, stirring constantly. Stir in broth and bring to simmer, stirring constantly. Add cream, reduce heat to medium, and simmer until sauce thickens, about 2-3 minutes. Season to taste with salt and pepper.

Assembly Instructions

  • Preheat the oven to 350°F. Place the green beans in a 9×13 baking dish. Pour the sauce over the beans. Top with an even layer of fried onions. Bake for 15-20 minutes, until the beans and sauce are hot and bubbling.

Video

Notes

This recipe can be prepared up to three days in advance. To prepare in advance and cook later, store the green beans wrapped in a paper towel in a gallon size freezer bag in the refrigerator. Store the sauce in an airtight container in the refrigerator. Set aside the fried onions until ready to serve.

Nutrition

📅 Oct 05, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Crispy tortillas and gooey cheese are paired with rich, savory mushrooms in this simple Mushroom Quesadilla. Add some spicy garlic and fresh parsley, and this is on its way to becoming a favorite quick lunch for me.

One of the things I love the most about quesadillas (and you know I LOVE quesadillas), is that they let even beginners transform simple ingredients into an absolutely mouth-watering snack or meal. After making Hungarian mushroom soup, I’d been looking for new ways to use up some of the leftover mushrooms. So, this mushroom quesadilla recipe was a no-brainer. A seriously delicious no-brainer.

Mushroom Quesadilla

My kids aren’t always fans of mushrooms, but every once in a while I find a recipe that leaves everyone coming back for seconds. And, these mushroom quesadillas do exactly that.

From start to finish, I can whip up a batch of them for my family in 20 minutes or so. Sometimes, one of the boys will help prep ingredients and it goes even faster. But, pretty much any dinner that I can have on the table in that amount of time (especially one that only dirties a single pan!) is a win in my book.

Ingredients and Substitutions

Mushrooms – I use fresh button or cremini mushrooms, because I like their flavor and it’s a breeze to slice them up and cook them.

Tortillas – I prefer flour tortillas for flavor and texture. You can use corn tortillas if you prefer.

Cheese – Monterey jack cheese is my go-to for quesadillas with more savory, complex flavors.

Butter – I prefer salted butter in all of my cooking unless specifically noted otherwise.

Garlic – I will always prefer fresh garlic. However, frozen garlic is often in my kitchen and it is my second choice here.

Parsley – Parsley provides a bright freshness that counters that earthiness of the mushrooms.

Salt & Pepper – I use kosher salt and freshly ground black pepper in almost all of my recipes.

Notes on Equipment

  • Chef’s knife. Food prep is so much easier when you have a quality knife, and this recipe is mostly prep. It was over 10 years ago when I first tried Victorinox knives, while visiting a professional test kitchen. You could say it was love at first slice. These are stellar knives, and the price point simply can’t be beat for quality. The 8″ has been in my kitchen ever since.
  • Cutting board
  • Measuring spoons
  • Spatula
  • Large nonstick skillet. I have been through about a million skillets over the years. Well, maybe not quite that many. But, I’ve tried pretty much everything, including some crazy expensive trendy brand names. And, for the past several years this All-Clad 12″ has been my everyday champ. It is also the pan I want for this one, since the tortilla has to release cleanly when it is time to flip.

How to Make a Mushroom Quesadilla

Cooking the Mushrooms

Prepping the Ingredients: I start by cleaning the mushrooms and then slicing them and chopping the parsley. You’ll also mince the garlic at this point.

Warming the Skillet: You’ll need to set a large non-stick skillet over medium-high heat on the stove for 2-3 minutes to heat it up fully.

Cooking the Mushrooms: Add the oil to the pan and then add the mushrooms. Toss to coat them in oil and then spread them out in an even layer. You’ll want to let them cook for about 3 minutes per side, flipping and stirring the mushrooms with a spatula. They should release and reabsorb their liquid, and develop a lovely brown crust.

Adding Butter and Garlic: Add 2 teaspoons of butter and the minced garlic to the pan and cook for another minute or so with the mushrooms, until fragrant.

Seasoning: I remove the pan from the heat and season with salt and pepper to taste. Transfer the finished mushrooms to a plate.

Making the Quesadilla

Cleaning the Skillet: I use a paper towel or cloth to wipe out the skillet before returning it to the pan at medium heat.

Buttering the Tortilla: I lightly butter one side of the tortilla. Then, I place it in the hot pan, butter-side down.

Filling the Quesadilla:  You’ll want to sprinkle half of the cheese on one side of the tortilla. Then spread mushrooms out over the cheese and sprinkle with parsley. Top with the remaining cheese.

Folding the Tortilla: Once all the filling ingredients have been added to the quesadilla, you’ll want to fold the tortilla over on itself and let cook for 2-3 minutes, until the cheese has begun to melt and the bottom tortilla is golden brown.

Flipping the Quesadilla: Flip the folded quesadilla over to cook the other side for another 2-3 minutes.

Serving: Once the quesadilla has browned on both sides, you can use a spatula to transfer the quesadilla to a cutting board. Slice into wedges and serve warm.

Expert Tip

If the tortilla is beginning to char before the cheese has fully melted, that’s a job for one of my favorite superheroes: Heat Management! Reduce the heat to medium-low and cover the skillet with a lid for about a minute. That will trap heat above the quesadilla to help melt the cheese while preventing it from burning.

Buttering the Tortilla – Taking a moment to spread butter directly on the tortilla gives you much more even browning than greasing the skillet, guaranteeing that perfect golden crispy texture all the way around. (Thanks to my sister Jenny for teaching me this trick!)

Slicing the Mushrooms – Make sure to take the time to finely slice the mushrooms when preparing the ingredients. I typically halve the mushrooms, then slice any large sections left over into uniform, quarter-inch pieces. This helps the mushrooms cook quickly and evenly in the skillet.

Fully Cooking Mushrooms – When you’re cooking the mushrooms by themselves, they will first release liquid into the pan, and then reabsorb it. You want to leave them on one side until they brown, then stir and let them brown again. You shouldn’t have to tend them more than a time or two. Once they have absorbed the liquid, you’ll be ready to add the butter and garlic.

Serving Suggestions

When I’m feeding more than one person, I like to turn these mushroom quesadillas into a whole spread. That means adding some protein options, like a fajita quesadilla and a steak quesadilla. The same pan can do it all.

And, you KNOW that if it’s my house, I’m going to be serving these with a mountain of tortilla chips. So, definitely going to include a bowl of queso fundido and pico de gallo for dipping.

Storage & Reheating

How to Store: Leftover quesadilla wedges can be stored in an airtight container or Ziploc in the refrigerator for 1-3 days.

How to Reheat: I typically reheat quesadillas in a skillet over medium heat, to restore any lost crispiness. You can also use the air fryer to rapidly bring them back up to temperature and help crisp them back up.

More Recipes for Mushroom Lovers

  • Chicken Tetrazzini
  • Buttered Steak Bites with Mushrooms
  • Scrambled Eggs with Oyster Mushrooms and Garlic Scapes
  • Mushroom and Red Pepper Rice Pilaf
  • Mushroom Jalapeno Salsa
  • Ground Beef Stroganoff

Frequently Asked Questions

How do I flip the quesadilla without spilling?

Getting better at temperature control and not overstuffing the tortilla made this less of an issue for me. If the cheese has melted, it’ll bind the mushrooms in place. It also helps to avoid over-stuffing the quesadilla. While I am a firm believer that you can never have too many mushrooms, I do have to admit that you CAN have too many in a single quesadilla. Don’t overload it and you’ll be fine.

How do I make sure the tortilla browns evenly?

Spreading butter directly on the tortilla gives you way better browning than greasing the skillet. It guarantees that perfect golden crispy texture all the way around.

What should I look for when choosing mushrooms?

When I’m shopping, I look for firm, unblemished mushrooms. They should never be shriveled, or discolored. Fresh mushrooms should be dry to the touch, with no soft or slimy spots, and smell earthy, never sour or musty.

Print

Mushroom Quesadilla

Make a tasty Mushroom Quesadilla with fresh mushrooms, garlic, butter, and melted jack cheese. One pan, twenty minutes, nothing wasted. Course Appetizer, Main Course Cuisine American Prep Time 5 minutes minutes Cook Time 15 minutes minutes Total Time 20 minutes minutes Calories 529kcal Author Mary Younkin

Ingredients

  • 2 teaspoons olive oil
  • 8 ounces fresh button or crimini mushrooms sliced thin or quartered
  • 1 tablespoon butter divided
  • 1 clove garlic finely minced
  • ¼ teaspoon kosher salt adjust to taste
  • ⅛ teaspoon freshly ground pepper adjust to taste
  • 1 tablespoon fresh Italian parsley finely chopped
  • 1 8-10" flour tortilla
  • ½ cup shredded Monterey Jack cheese

Instructions

  • Warm a large nonstick skillet over medium heat for 1-2 minutes. Add the olive oil to the pan and then add the mushrooms to the pan. Toss to coat the mushrooms in the oil and then spread them across the pan in a layer. Cook for about 3 minutes on one side. Stir and continue cooking while stirring occasionally for an additional 3-4 more minutes.
  • Cook until all liquid has been absorbed and the mushroom edges have browned. Add about 2 teaspoons of butter to the pan along with the garlic. Toss the mushrooms in the butter and garlic and continue cooking for an additional minute, until the butter and garlic have been absorbed and the pan is fragrant. Remove from the heat and season to taste with salt and pepper. Transfer to a plate.
  • Wipe out the skillet with a paper towel or cloth and return to the stove over medium heat. Lightly butter one side of the tortilla with the remaining teaspoon of butter and place it in the hot pan. Top half of the tortilla with about half of the cheese. Spread the mushrooms across the cheese. Sprinkle with parsley.
  • Sprinkle the rest of the cheese over the mushrooms, and fold the tortilla over on itself. Let it cook for 2-3 minutes. When the cheese has begun to melt and the bottom tortilla is browning slightly, flip the folded quesadilla over to cook the other side.
  • Continue to cook until all the cheese is melted and the tortilla is crisp around the edges. Using a spatula, transfer the quesadilla back to a cutting board and cut into wedges while it is hot, and serve warm. Repeat as desired.

Nutrition

Calories: 529kcal | Carbohydrates: 24g | Protein: 24g | Fat: 40g | Saturated Fat: 20g | Polyunsaturated Fat: 3g | Monounsaturated Fat: 14g | Trans Fat: 0.5g | Cholesterol: 80mg | Sodium: 1245mg | Potassium: 842mg | Fiber: 4g | Sugar: 6g | Vitamin A: 1122IU | Vitamin C: 11mg | Calcium: 487mg | Iron: 3mg
📅 Oct 03, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

No-Bake Caramel Apple Lush is an easy fall dessert with layers of cinnamon graham cracker crust, creamy cheesecake filling, butterscotch apple pie filling, and a fluffy Cool Whip topping. Finished with caramel sauce and butterscotch chips, it has all the flavors of a caramel apple in a cool and creamy dessert. Best of all, no oven required.

This recipe is a great make-ahead dessert for Thanksgiving, potlucks, and fall gatherings and it is one of my favorite apple recipes. It’s made in a 9×13-inch baking dish, so there’s plenty to share.

Why You’ll Love This Caramel Apple Lush

  • No baking required. Perfect for when you don’t want to turn the oven on.
  • Easy shortcut ingredients. Canned apple pie filling, instant butterscotch pudding, and Cool Whip make it incredibly simple.
  • Perfect fall flavors. Apple, caramel, cinnamon, and butterscotch are such a good combination.
  • Great for a crowd. It’s made in a 9×13-inch pan and can serve 12 people easily.
  • Make-ahead friendly. It needs at least 2 hours to chill, making it ideal for entertaining.
Want to save this recipe? Just enter your email and get it sent to your inbox! Plus you’ll get new recipes from us every week!Please enable JavaScript in your browser to complete this form. Please enable JavaScript in your browser to complete this form. Name * Email * Save

4 Delicious Layers

  1. Graham Cracker Layer– I use cinnamon graham crackers, but you can use plain graham crackers and add a little ground cinnamon.
  2. Cream Cheese Layer– this layer is wonderfully creamy and rich and is a simple mixture of cream cheese, powdered sugar, and Cool Whip.
  3. Apple Pie/Butterscotch Pudding Layer– this layer is where the flavor really comes in. It only has 3 ingredients but really packs the flavor. You’ll whisk together a box of instant butterscotch pudding mix with 1 1/2 cups cold milk and then stir in a can of apple pie filling. The butterscotch pudding flavor is really similar to caramel and really complements the apples.
  4. Cool Whip Topping– It wouldn’t be a layer dessert without a cool and creamy Cool Whip Topping.

Finish it off with some butterscotch chips and a caramel drizzle for even more flavor.

Recommended Equipment

  • 9×13-inch baking dish– I love to use a glass baking dish to show off the layers.
  • Hand-Held Electric Mixer– I get a lot of use out of my trusty KitchenAid Hand-Held Mixer.

No-Bake Caramel Apple Lush Recipe Tip

Be sure the cream cheese is softened or you’ll have a difficult time getting it smooth.

Want To Add Some Crunch?

Sprinkle toasted chopped pecans or walnuts on top.

Make Ahead and Storage

Should be made at least 2 hours in advance and can be made a day in advance. Leftovers will keep for about 3 days in the refrigerator if covered well.
